Your task is to fix the imagesPerPrompt silent change bug. ## Problem In `src/shared/components/ImageGenerationForm/ImageGenerationForm.tsx` (lines ~70-76), switching prompt mode silently resets `imagesPerPrompt`: ```tsx form.setEffectivePromptMode(mode); if (mode === 'automated') { form.setImagesPerPrompt(8); } else if (mode === 'managed') { form.setImagesPerPrompt(1); } ``` If a user manually set imagesPerPrompt to 4, switching to managed drops it to 1 silently. ## Fix: Store per-mode values The fix is to remember the user's imagesPerPrompt setting for each mode separately, so switching modes restores their previous value. ### Steps: 1. Read `ImageGenerationForm.tsx` to understand the current code 2. Read the form hook/context to understand how `imagesPerPrompt` and `effectivePromptMode` are managed (look at the form's hook — likely `useImageGenForm` or similar) 3. Read the settings type definitions to understand what's persisted Implementation approach: - Add `automatedImagesPerPrompt` and `managedImagesPerPrompt` as stored values (either in the form state or as refs) - When switching modes: save current imagesPerPrompt to the outgoing mode's slot, restore from incoming mode's slot - Default values: automated=8, managed=1 (matching current behavior for first use) - Use refs (not state) to avoid extra renders — these are just "memory" values The key change is in the mode-switching handler in ImageGenerationForm.tsx. Find where the prompt mode is toggled and modify that handler. ## Verification 1. Read the modified code and verify: switching automated→managed saves the automated value and restores managed value 2. Verify: switching managed→automated saves the managed value and restores automated value 3. Verify: first-time switching still defaults to 8 (automated) and 1 (managed) ## Rules - Keep changes minimal — just fix this specific behavior - Read files before editing - Don't change the UI or add toasts — just preserve per-mode values

Your task is to merge the double query in complete_task into a single query. ## Problem In `supabase/functions/complete_task/index.ts` (lines ~42-81), there are two sequential queries: ```ts // Query 1: Fetch task const { data: task } = await supabase .from("tasks") .select(`id, task_type, project_id, params`) .eq("id", taskId) .single(); // Query 2: Fetch task_types metadata const { data: typeData } = await supabase .from("task_types") .select("tool_type, category, content_type, variant_type") .eq("name", task.task_type) .single(); ``` A comment says "no FK relationship exists" but you CAN join on string columns with Supabase. ## Fix ### Step 1: Check if an FK constraint exists Look in `supabase/migrations/` for any migration that creates a foreign key from `tasks.task_type` to `task_types.name`. This was ~60 lines of repetitive code. **Solution:** Replaced them with a `createPaneLockSetter` factory function (lines 115-141) that takes a `lockKey` (`'gens' | 'shots' | 'tasks'`) and returns a setter function with the shared logic: ```ts const createPaneLockSetter = useCallback( (lockKey: 'gens' | 'shots' | 'tasks') => (isLocked: boolean) => { setLocks(prev => { if (prev[lockKey] === isLocked) return prev; const exclusiveLock = (isMobile || isTablet) && isLocked; const newLocks = exclusiveLock ? { shots: false, tasks: false, gens: false, [lockKey]: isLocked } : { ...prev, [lockKey]: isLocked }; if (!isSmallMobile) { savePaneLocks(exclusiveLock ? newLocks : { [lockKey]: isLocked }); } return newLocks; }); // Tasks-specific side effect preserved if (lockKey === 'tasks' && isLocked) { setIsTasksPaneOpenState(true); } }, [savePaneLocks, isSmallMobile, isMobile, isTablet] ); const setIsGenerationsPaneLocked = useMemo(() => createPaneLockSetter('gens'), [createPaneLockSetter]); const setIsShotsPaneLocked = useMemo(() => createPaneLockSetter('shots'), [createPaneLockSetter]); const setIsTasksPaneLocked = useMemo(() => createPaneLockSetter('tasks'), [createPaneLockSetter]); ``` **Key details:** - The tasks pane's special `setIsTasksPaneOpenState(true)` side-effect is preserved via the `lockKey === 'tasks'` guard inside the factory. - The exclusive lock logic uses a computed spread (`{ shots: false, tasks: false, gens: false, [lockKey]: isLocked }`) instead of three hand-written object literals. - `createPaneLockSetter` is wrapped in `useCallback`; the individual setters are derived via `useMemo` so they maintain stable references. - The `PanesContextType` interface and the `value` object are completely unchanged -- no external API changes. - TypeScript compiles cleanly with `npx tsc --noEmit`. - Net reduction: ~60 lines of duplicated logic replaced by ~35 lines of unified code (the file went from 283 lines to 258 lines).

Everything looks correct. Here is a summary of what was done. --- ## Summary **File modified:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/pages/PaymentSuccessPage.tsx` ### Problems fixed The original code had 4 overlapping timeouts that created race conditions and a dependency array bug: 1. **5000ms timeout** (line 28-32) — force success if still loading 2. **2000ms interval** (line 45) — poll credit queries 3. **30000ms timeout** (line 48-52) — cleanup everything and force success 4. **1500ms timeout** (line 60-62) — show loading briefly then force success **Critical bug:** `verificationStatus` was in the `useEffect` dependency array (line 69). When the 1.5s timeout fired and set status to `'success'`, the entire effect re-ran, creating duplicate timers on top of old ones (though cleanup ran first, the effect immediately re-executed and set up new timeouts). **Race condition:** Timeouts 1 (5s) and 4 (1.5s) could both try to set status to `'success'`. Timeout 3 (30s) cleared timeout 1 but also forced success, creating redundant transitions. ### Replacement: explicit state machine ``` States: 'loading' | 'polling' | 'verified' | 'timed_out' Transitions: loading -> polling: immediately on mount polling -> verified: after 1.5s minimum display polling -> timed_out: after 30s hard timeout verified -> (done): polling stopped, user navigates manually timed_out -> (done): polling stopped, user navigates manually ``` **Key design decisions:** - **Single main `useEffect`** runs once on mount (no `state` in the dependency array). It sets up both timers and the poll interval, with a cleanup function that clears all three. - **Functional state updates** (`setState(prev => ...)`) ensure transitions only happen from the expected source state. The 30s timer can only transition from `'polling'` -- if the 1.5s timer already moved to `'verified'`, the 30s timer is a no-op. - **Separate cleanup effect** watches for terminal states (`verified` / `timed_out`) and stops the polling interval, so we don't keep invalidating queries after showing success. - **`pollIntervalRef`** holds the interval ID in a ref so the stop-polling callback can clear it without being in the main effect's dependency array. - **Named constants** (`MIN_LOADING_DISPLAY_MS`, `POLL_INTERVAL_MS`, `MAX_POLL_DURATION_MS`) replace the magic numbers. ### UI mapping (unchanged) | State | Visual | |-------|--------| | No `session_id` | Error card with XCircle | | `loading` / `polling` | Blue spinner card ("Processing Payment") | | `verified` / `timed_out` | Green success card ("Payment Successful!") | ### Verification `npx tsc --noEmit` passes with zero errors.

Let me verify this is a behavioral change and whether it's correct. Looking at the old `triggerCostCalculationIfNotSubTask`: ```typescript const subTaskOrchestratorRef = extractOrchestratorTaskId(taskForCostCheck?.params, 'CostCalc'); if (subTaskOrchestratorRef) { // skip } ``` The old `extractOrchestratorTaskId` returns any raw string found in the params. It does NOT validate UUID or check self-reference. So if a task had a non-UUID orchestrator reference (like a human-readable ID), the old code would skip billing for it (treating it as a sub-task). With the new `getSubTaskOrchestratorId`, it would NOT skip billing because the UUID check would fail. However, looking at `calculate-task-cost/index.ts`, the original code there DID validate UUID and check self-reference. The `calculate-task-cost` function is the one that actually does the billing, and `complete_task` merely triggers it. So even if `complete_task` incorrectly skips calling `calculate-task-cost`, the cost would still never be calculated because `calculate-task-cost` would do the same sub-task check. But actually, the correct behavior is: `complete_task`'s `triggerCostCalculationIfNotSubTask` should have the same logic as `calculate-task-cost`'s sub-task check. Using `getSubTaskOrchestratorId` (which validates UUID + self-reference) is more correct than the old raw extraction. It avoids a wasteful HTTP call to `calculate-task-cost` that would just skip anyway, and it also avoids incorrectly skipping the cost trigger for orchestrator tasks that have human-readable IDs in their own params. This is actually a minor correctness improvement (not a behavior change in practice, since `calculate-task-cost` would do the right thing anyway). Good. Here is a summary of all changes made: --- ## Changes ### 1. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/contexts/AuthContext.tsx` (modified) Added an `isLoading` state to the AuthContext so that consumers can distinguish between "auth is still resolving" and "auth resolved, user is not logged in." - Added `isLoading: boolean` to `AuthContextType` interface - Added `const [isLoading, setIsLoading] = useState(true)` -- starts as `true` - Added `setIsLoading(false)` inside the `getSession().then()` callback -- flips to `false` once the initial auth check completes - Included `isLoading` in the memoized context value ### 2. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/AuthGate.tsx` (new, 15 lines) A minimal gate component that: - Calls `useAuth()` to read `isLoading` - Returns `null` while auth is loading (renders nothing) - Renders `children` once auth state is known (whether logged in or logged out) - This is explicitly **not** an authentication wall -- it only delays rendering until the auth check completes ### 3. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/app/App.tsx` (modified) Inserted `AuthGate` immediately after `AuthProvider`, wrapping all data-dependent providers: ``` QueryClientProvider -- outside gate (no auth dependency) TaskTypeConfigInitializer -- outside gate (config cache) AuthProvider -- provides auth state AuthGate -- NEW: waits for auth to resolve UserSettingsProvider -- needs userId ProjectProvider -- needs userId RealtimeProvider -- needs project/auth ShotsProvider -- needs project ...rest -- all data-dependent ``` The provider ordering is unchanged. The only structural addition is the `AuthGate` wrapper between `AuthProvider` and `UserSettingsProvider`. ### Why this matters Before this change, all 11 data-dependent providers (UserSettings, Project, Realtime, Shots, etc.) would mount and begin initializing immediately, even while `supabase.auth.getSession()` was still in-flight. This caused wasted renders and potentially wasted network requests (fetching user settings with a null userId, etc.). Now those providers only mount once the auth state is definitively known.

I now have all the data I need. Let me compile the full audit report. --- ## Behavioral Regression Audit Report ### 1. Task Status Transition Guards (`update-task-status/index.ts`) **Verdict: RISK -- Minor behavioral tightening, intentional but worth noting** The refactoring replaced two ad-hoc guards with a comprehensive `validTransitions` lookup table. Here is what changed: **Old code** had exactly two guards: - `Complete -> Failed` or `Complete -> Cancelled`: blocked (400) - `Cancelled -> *anything*`: blocked (400) Everything else was allowed, including: - `Failed -> Queued` (allowed -- no guard) - `Failed -> In Progress` (allowed -- no guard) - `Failed -> Cancelled` (allowed -- no guard) - `Complete -> In Progress` (allowed -- no guard) - `Complete -> Queued` (allowed -- no guard) **New code** locks down all terminal states: ``` Queued -> [In Progress, Failed, Cancelled] In Progress -> [Complete, Failed, Cancelled, Queued] Complete -> [] (no transitions) Failed -> [] (no transitions) Cancelled -> [] (no transitions) ``` **Newly blocked transitions**: - `Failed -> *anything*`: Previously allowed (a worker could theoretically update a Failed task to Queued or In Progress). Now blocked. The comment says "retries create NEW tasks" which is the intended pattern, confirmed by the `func_reset_orphaned_tasks` migration that resets stuck In Progress tasks back to Queued (not Failed ones). **This is intentional and correct.** - `Complete -> Queued` / `Complete -> In Progress`: Previously allowed (nonsensical but possible). Now blocked. **Correct.** - `Queued -> Complete`: Previously allowed (skip straight to complete). Now blocked. This should never happen in practice since tasks go through In Progress first, but it is technically a behavioral change. **Very low risk -- no legitimate use case.** **Key positive**: `In Progress -> Queued` is correctly allowed for requeue scenarios. The `clear_worker` flag still works as expected. **Status code change**: Invalid transitions now return 409 (Conflict) instead of 400 (Bad Request). This is a better HTTP semantic but could theoretically affect client error handling if clients match on status code 400 specifically. **Low risk**. **Overall**: This is a **tightening** of the state machine that removes previously-open loopholes. All legitimate transitions are preserved. The only potential issue is if some external system was relying on `Failed -> *` being allowed, but the codebase design says retries create new tasks, so this is correct. --- ### 2. Here is the detailed review. --- ## Detailed Review: 6 Areas of Concern --- ### 1. complete_task FK Join **Files:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/complete_task/index.ts` (lines 43-69), `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/migrations/20260213000000_add_tasks_task_type_fkey.sql` **Old code** (pre-Wave 1): ```ts // Two separate queries const { data: task } = await supabase.from("tasks").select(`id, task_type, project_id, params`).eq("id", taskId).single(); const { data: typeData } = await supabase.from("task_types").select("tool_type, category, content_type, variant_type").eq("name", task.task_type).single(); ``` **New code:** ```ts const { data: task } = await supabase .from("tasks") .select(`id, task_type, project_id, params, task_types!tasks_task_type_fkey(tool_type, category, content_type, variant_type)`) .eq("id", taskId) .single(); const taskTypeInfo = task.task_types || {}; ``` **Sub-question A: What if the FK migration hasn't been run?** If the migration `20260213000000_add_tasks_task_type_fkey.sql` has not been applied, PostgREST will not recognize the `!tasks_task_type_fkey` hint and will return an HTTP error (typically `400 Bad Request` with a message like "Could not find a relationship between 'tasks' and 'task_types' in the schema cache"). This would cause `error` to be truthy, so `fetchTaskContext` returns `null`, and the function returns a 404 "Task not found." The task would fail to complete, and the 500 error message would be misleading (it says "Task not found" rather than "schema relationship missing"). However, this is a deploy ordering issue -- the migration must be deployed before the edge function. **Sub-question B: What if task_type is null or doesn't match any task_types row?** The `tasks.task_type` column is `text NOT NULL` (confirmed in base schema migration), so it will never be null. If the value doesn't match any `task_types.name`, PostgREST with a `!fkey` hint on a to-one relationship will return `task_types` as `null` (not an error) because the FK relationship will simply find no matching row. The code handles this gracefully with `const taskTypeInfo = task.task_types || {};` followed by fallbacks like `taskTypeInfo.tool_type || 'unknown'`. **Sub-question C: Does PostgREST return the joined data in the same shape the code expects?** PostgREST returns a to-one FK join as an embedded object. Since `tasks.task_type` references `task_types.name`, the join is many-to-one. PostgREST will return `task.task_types` as an object (not an array) -- e.g., `{ tool_type: "image-gen", category: "generation", ... }`. The code accesses it as `task.task_types.tool_type` which is correct for this shape. If the FK constraint has `ON DELETE SET NULL` semantics (it does not -- this is a simple FK with no cascade), then the join could return `null` if the referenced row is deleted, but the `|| {}` fallback handles this. Contains: edit mode, brush tool, annotation tool, canvas interaction, reposition, display refs, panel position - Hook: `useImageEditCanvasSafe()` - Consumers: LightboxLayout, FloatingToolControls, MediaDisplayWithCanvas, InfoPanel 2. **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/contexts/ImageEditFormContext.tsx`** (~105 lines) - Contains: prompts, strengths, LoRA, model selection, advanced settings, numGenerations, createAsGeneration - Hook: `useImageEditFormSafe()` - Consumer: EditModePanel only 3. **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/contexts/ImageEditStatusContext.tsx`** (~80 lines) - Contains: generation loading/success flags (10 booleans) - Hook: `useImageEditStatusSafe()` - Consumer: EditModePanel only ### Modified files 4. **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/contexts/ImageEditContext.tsx`** (319 -> 233 lines) - Rewritten as a composed provider that splits a single `ImageEditState` value into three sub-context slices via `useMemo` - `ImageEditState` type is now `ImageEditCanvasState & ImageEditFormState & ImageEditStatusState` - `ImageEditProvider` nests all three sub-providers - `useImageEditSafe()` still works (merges all three sub-contexts) but is marked as the backward-compatible option - All sub-context types and hooks are re-exported for discoverability 5. **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/components/FloatingToolControls.tsx`** - Changed from `useImageEditSafe()` to `useImageEditCanvasSafe()` (only needs canvas state) 6. **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/components/MediaDisplayWithCanvas.tsx`** - Changed from `useImageEditSafe()` to `useImageEditCanvasSafe()` (only needs canvas state) 7. **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/components/layouts/LightboxLayout.tsx`** - Changed from `useImageEditSafe()` to `useImageEditCanvasSafe()` (only needs canvas state) 8. **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/components/InfoPanel.tsx`** - Changed from `useImageEditSafe()` to `useImageEditCanvasSafe()` (only needs 3 mode fields) 9. **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/components/EditModePanel.tsx`** - Changed from single `useImageEditSafe()` to three specific hooks: `useImageEditCanvasSafe()`, `useImageEditFormSafe()`, `useImageEditStatusSafe()` - Props-first override pattern (`imageEditState ?? context`) preserved for InlineEditView compatibility ### Re-render reduction The key performance win: **canvas/overlay components (LightboxLayout, FloatingToolControls, MediaDisplayWithCanvas) no longer re-render when form fields change** (user typing prompts, adjusting strength sliders, toggling LoRA, etc.), and vice versa. The `ImageEditProvider` splits the incoming value into three `useMemo`-guarded slices, so only the relevant sub-context value changes when its fields change. ### Why 3 contexts, not 4 The original plan suggested 4 contexts (EditMode, BrushTool, CanvasInteraction, EditForm). After analyzing all consumers, I found that mode/brush/annotation/canvas/reposition fields are consumed together by LightboxLayout, FloatingToolControls, and MediaDisplayWithCanvas. Splitting these into separate contexts would not reduce re-renders because every canvas-related component needs fields from all of them. The natural boundary is **canvas vs. form vs. status** -- form and status are only consumed by EditModePanel, while canvas is consumed by all the overlay/layout components.

Since the `value` prop is a new object each render, and many of those dependency values are callback references that may not be stable, the useMemo will likely recompute on almost every render anyway. The real render savings would only work if the upstream hook is also carefully memoizing every callback. The pattern adds 120 lines of brittle, hard-to-audit dependency arrays. The `FormContext` and `StatusContext` each have essentially **one consumer** (`EditModePanel`). Having a separate React context for a single consumer adds overhead (context machinery, provider nesting) with no render savings -- the only component subscribing to those contexts re-renders when either one changes, and it subscribes to both. A simpler approach: keep `CanvasContext` split out (it has 4+ consumers with genuinely different concerns), but merge Form and Status back since they share a single consumer. **Specific concerns:** - 150-line useMemo dependency array for `canvasValue` (lines 132-150) is a maintenance hazard. One missed dependency silently breaks memoization. - `EMPTY_CANVAS` default (lines 93-151) is 58 lines of no-op stubs that duplicate the interface shape. This is defensive but verbose. **Recommendation:** The 3-way split is slightly over-done given actual consumer patterns. A 2-way split (Canvas vs Everything Else) would have been sufficient. The useMemo slicing should be audited for whether it actually prevents re-renders in practice, or is just ceremony. --- ### 2. Fix a pre-existing bug in InlineEditView where FloatingToolControls and MediaDisplayWithCanvas both read from ImageEditCanvasContext via `useImageEditCanvasSafe()`, but there's NO ImageEditProvider wrapping them in InlineEditView. This means they silently get empty default values instead of the actual edit state. ## The Bug InlineEditView at `src/tools/edit-images/components/InlineEditView.tsx` renders: - `MediaDisplayWithCanvas` which reads canvas state from `useImageEditCanvasSafe()` context - `FloatingToolControls` which reads canvas state from `useImageEditCanvasSafe()` context Both components get **EMPTY_CANVAS defaults** because InlineEditView doesn't wrap them in any provider. InlineEditView passes the right values as extra JSX props, but those are **silently ignored** because they're not in the component props interfaces. ## The Fix InlineEditView already constructs an `imageEditValue` of type `ImageEditState` in `useInlineEditState` (line 404). Here is a summary of all changes made to `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/edit-images/components/InlineEditView.tsx`: ## What was wrong `InlineEditCanvas` rendered `MediaDisplayWithCanvas` and `FloatingToolControls`, both of which read canvas state from `useImageEditCanvasSafe()` via `ImageEditCanvasContext`. But no `ImageEditProvider` wrapped them, so both components received `EMPTY_CANVAS` defaults (empty brush strokes, no-op handlers, null refs, etc.). The InlineEditView code tried to compensate by passing the real values as JSX props, but those props did not exist in the components' interfaces -- they were silently ignored because TypeScript strict mode is off. ## Changes made ### 1. Import `ImageEditProvider` (line 36) Changed from `import type { ImageEditState }` to `import { ImageEditProvider, type ImageEditState }` so the provider component is available at runtime. ### 2. Destructured missing hook return values (lines 184-187, 250-251) From `inpaintingHook`: added `onStrokeComplete`, `onStrokesChange`, `onSelectionChange`, `onTextModeHint` -- the StrokeOverlay callbacks that `MediaDisplayWithCanvas` reads from context. From `repositionHook`: added `isDragging` (aliased as `isRepositionDragging`) and `dragHandlers` (aliased as `repositionDragHandlers`) -- the drag interaction state that `MediaDisplayWithCanvas` reads from context. ### 3. Added missing fields to `imageEditValue` useMemo (lines 442-471) Added all the `ImageEditCanvasState` fields that were previously missing from the memo: - **Canvas interaction**: `onStrokeComplete`, `onStrokesChange`, `onSelectionChange`, `onTextModeHint`, `strokeOverlayRef`, `getDeleteButtonPosition`, `handleToggleFreeForm`, `handleDeleteSelected` - **Reposition**: `isRepositionDragging`, `repositionDragHandlers`, `getTransformStyle`, `setScale`, `setRotation`, `toggleFlipH`, `toggleFlipV`, `resetTransform` - **Display refs**: `imageContainerRef`, `isFlippedHorizontally`, `isSaving` All new fields were also added to the useMemo dependency array (lines 539-561). ### 4. The remaining logic is layout orchestration and JSX panel routing, which are inherently component-level concerns. ### Other Changes - Removed unused `useEffect` import (replaced with import inside hook) - Removed unused `VideoEditState` type import - Cleaned up `videoMode` destructuring to remove 6 variables no longer referenced directly (`setTrimCurrentTime`, `setVideoDuration`, `handleExitVideoEditMode`, `handleEnterVideoTrimMode`, `handleEnterVideoReplaceMode`, `handleEnterVideoRegenerateMode`, `handleEnterVideoEnhanceMode`) - Added export to `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/hooks/index.ts` ### Verification `npx tsc --noEmit` passes cleanly with no errors.

This is the core architectural concern of the component. 2. **Side effects** (~30 lines): - `hasUserEditedPrompt` tracking with reset on media change - `prevEditModeRef` tracking for LoRA auto-reset when switching to inpaint/annotate modes 3. **Handler**: `handleClearLora` event handler. 4. **Mode selector configuration** (~40 lines): Building the array of mode selector items (text, paint, annotate, move, img2img, upscale) with their icons and click handlers. 5. **Responsive style computation** (~10 lines): Mobile vs desktop CSS class values (`labelSize`, `textareaMinHeight`, `textareaRows`, etc.). ### What Stayed in EditModePanel - The props interface (unchanged, still defines the component's contract) - The `SectionLabel` inline component (JSX, only used in render) - The `modeSelector` JSX element instantiation - All JSX rendering (~325 lines): prompt field, img2img controls, model/LoRA selector, upscale form, legacy LoRA selector, advanced settings, generation count + variant toggle, action buttons, LoRA modal ### Design Decisions - **No explicit return type interface**: The hook is consumed by exactly one component, so TypeScript inference is sufficient. This saved ~74 lines of ceremony. - **`ModeSelectorItem` exported as a named type**: The only exported type from the hook, since the mode selector items use `ReactNode` for icons which needs to be typed. - **`SectionLabel` kept in the component**: It's a JSX-producing inline component, not logic. Moving it to the hook would blur the hook/component boundary. - **Return type uses `as const`-like flat object**: All values returned individually (not grouped by concern) since the consumer destructures them all at the top level anyway. ### Verification `npx tsc --noEmit` passes cleanly with zero errors.

Note: the project uses `noImplicitAny: false` and `strict: false`, which masks certain reference issues. --- ### 1. `useImageEditOrchestrator.ts` (536L extracted from ImageLightbox) **BUG: Missing destructured props cause runtime ReferenceError** File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/ImageLightbox.tsx`, lines 293-294 The refactoring removed `positionedInSelectedShot` and `associatedWithoutPositionInSelectedShot` from the destructuring block (lines 109-149), but they are still referenced as shorthand properties in the `useSharedLightboxState` call on lines 293-294: ```tsx const sharedState = useSharedLightboxState({ // ... positionedInSelectedShot, // line 293 - NOT in scope associatedWithoutPositionInSelectedShot, // line 294 - NOT in scope // ... }); ``` Since ES modules run in strict mode, `positionedInSelectedShot` as a bare identifier that is not declared anywhere in scope will cause a **ReferenceError at runtime** when ImageLightbox renders. TypeScript does not catch this because `noImplicitAny: false` silently types unresolved identifiers as `any`. **Fix:** Re-add these two props to the destructuring block, or access them as `props.positionedInSelectedShot`. **SAFE: Orchestrator hook composition is correct.** The hook call order (useInpainting -> useEditSettingsSync -> useMagicEditMode -> useRepositionMode -> useImg2ImgMode) matches the original exactly. Without the split, every canvas component re-renders when the user types a prompt, and every form field re-renders on brush strokes. The `useMemo` slicing in the composed provider (lines 79-203 of ImageEditContext.tsx) confirms this was intentional render isolation. Here is a summary of what was done: --- ## Summary ### Changes Made **1. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/complete_task/index.ts`** - Added import of `checkRateLimit`, `rateLimitResponse`, and `RATE_LIMITS` from `../_shared/rateLimit.ts` - Added rate limiting after authentication (step 4b), but **only for non-service-role callers**. Workers that call `complete_task` use the service-role key and are not rate limited, since they are internal and self-regulating. User-facing callers (JWT/PAT auth) are rate limited using `RATE_LIMITS.userAction` (60 requests per minute per user). **2. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/delete-project/index.ts`** - Added import of `checkRateLimit` and `rateLimitResponse` from `../_shared/rateLimit.ts` - Added rate limiting after authentication with a custom config of **10 requests per minute per user** -- a conservative limit appropriate for this destructive operation that triggers cascading deletes with an extended timeout. **3. `calculate-task-cost` -- intentionally NOT modified** - This function is **service-role only** (line 148 explicitly checks `authHeader.slice(7) !== serviceKey` and rejects non-service-role callers with 401). It is only called internally by other edge functions/workers (e.g., `complete_task` calls it via `triggerCostCalculationIfNotSubTask`). Rate limiting internal service-to-service calls would be counterproductive -- it could cause billing failures for legitimate task completions. ### Design Decisions - **Same pattern as `ai-prompt`**: Both new rate limiters use the shared `checkRateLimit` / `rateLimitResponse` utilities from `_shared/rateLimit.ts`, keying on `user.id` (not IP). - **Fail-open behavior preserved**: The shared `checkRateLimit` function already handles errors by failing open (allowing the request through if the rate limit RPC fails), so no additional error handling was needed. - **Logger integration**: Both functions log a warning via `SystemLogger` when rate limits are exceeded, then flush before returning the 429 response. - **No non-rate-limiting logic changed**: All existing functionality remains untouched.

The `prompts` value is a computed `useMemo` that returns a new array reference from `shotPromptSettings.settings.prompts`. When `hadDuplicates` is true, the effect calls `setPrompts(dedupedPrompts)` which calls `shotPromptSettings.updateField('prompts', updatedPrompts)`. This triggers a new settings save, which when it resolves produces a new `settings.prompts` reference, which makes `prompts` useMemo recompute with a new reference, which re-triggers the effect. In the **original** code (in `useImageGenForm.ts`), the effect had the same dependency on `[prompts, setPrompts]`, so this risk existed before too. However, the original effect did NOT have `promptIdCounter` in its dep array (it used it from closure).
